It's just... it's like the old Ruben, the guy who could talk to anybody and actually enjoy his day, is just gone.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It's been... about eight months now. Yeah, eight months. It really started when the new regional manager took over. He just started coming down on me... taking away accounts that I’d handled for years.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Not anymore, no. I used to... well, I used to really look forward to things. Bowling, working out in the garden, taking my wife to gallery shows, or going dancing. But now... I just sit there. I find myself making excuses not to go out because, honestly, none of it feels worth the effort anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about harming yourself or wished you were dead during these past eight months?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I have. I've been having these thoughts that maybe my wife and kids would just be better off if I didn't wake up in the morning. It's hard to say out loud, but... those thoughts really scare me. They scare me enough that I honestly just don't trust myself the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange or unusual thoughts, such as hearing voices or seeing things that others cannot, during these past eight months?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I haven't heard any voices or seen anything that isn't there. It's really just... just my own thoughts getting so dark. But no, no strange visions or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your appetite or sleep patterns over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, my sleep has been... well, it's been pretty bad. At night I just lie there staring at the ceiling, replaying work over and over in my head. And when I finally do get to sleep, I end up waking up around three or four in the morning. I wake up already worn out... just dreading having to get through another day. \n\nAs for my appetite, I haven't really noticed much of a change there, honestly. It's mostly just the sleeping that's a problem.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant loss of energy or fatigue over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, like I just mentioned, I'm tired when I wake up because I'm not sleeping well. But other than that... I haven't noticed any real drop in my physical energy. I don't feel unusually fatigued during the day or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a significant reduction in self-esteem over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. I just... I keep telling myself I’m useless now. It's like all those years of taking care of my clients and providing for my family, it feels like none of that even counts anymore. And it's just because I can’t even handle this job right now. So, yeah, I guess you could say my confidence is pretty much gone. I just feel worthless.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of worthlessness and the thoughts of your family being better off without you are present most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say so. It doesn't really let up.\n\nDoctor: Would you say these feelings and thoughts have been present every day for the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's been every day. It's been going on for about eight months now. It all really started when the new regional manager came in and just started coming down on me, taking away accounts that I'd handled for years. Ever since then, it's just been every single day.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any psychomotor agitation or retardation over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: I'm not exactly sure what those words mean, to be honest. Um... if you're asking if I'm moving unusually slow, or if I'm, you know, restless and pacing around all the time... no, I haven't really noticed anything like that. Physically, I'm moving about the same as I always have.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. But no, no strange visions or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your appetite or sleep patterns over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, my sleep has been... well, it's been pretty bad. At night I just lie there staring at the ceiling, replaying work over and over in my head. And when I finally do get to sleep, I end up waking up around three or four in the morning. I wake up already worn out... just dreading having to get through another day. \n\nAs for my appetite, I haven't really noticed much of a change there, honestly. It's mostly just the sleeping that's a problem.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant loss of energy or fatigue over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, like I just mentioned, I'm tired when I wake up because I'm not sleeping well. But other than that... I haven't noticed any real drop in my physical energy. I don't feel unusually fatigued during the day or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a significant reduction in self-esteem over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. I just... I keep telling myself I’m useless now. It's like all those years of taking care of my clients and providing for my family, it feels like none of that even counts anymore. And it's just because I can’t even handle this job right now. So, yeah, I guess you could say my confidence is pretty much gone. I just feel worthless.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of worthlessness and the thoughts of your family being better off without you are present most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say so. It's pretty much a constant thing. From the time I wake up in the morning to when I'm trying to fall asleep at night, those thoughts are just... they're just there. It doesn't really let up.\n\nDoctor: Would you say these feelings and thoughts have been present every day for the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's been every day. It's been going on for about eight months now. It all really started when the new regional manager came in and just started coming down on me, taking away accounts that I'd handled for years. Ever since then, it's just been every single day.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any psychomotor agitation or retardation over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: I'm not exactly sure what those words mean, to be honest. Um... if you're asking if I'm moving unusually slow, or if I'm, you know, restless and pacing around all the time... no, I haven't really noticed anything like that. Physically, I'm moving about the same as I always have.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of depression have caused you significant distress or impairment in your social, occupational, or other areas of functioning?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really has. At work, I actually missed an important client presentation recently. I just couldn't get it together. And at home... I've pulled back so much from everything. Honestly, my wife is the one who felt she had to bring me in here to see you. She said she didn't think I was safe carrying on like this anymore.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your depression is moderately severe, severe, or extremely severe, based on how much it affects your daily life and ability to function?\n\nPatient: I guess I would say it's severe. It's really affecting everything I do, like I said about work and my wife bringing me in. It's definitely not just a mild thing... it feels pretty severe to me.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in, even if it's just a little bit?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I used to look forward to a lot of things... bowling, working out in the garden, taking my wife to gallery shows, or even just going dancing. But now... I just don't get any enjoyment out of them. I wake up already worn out... just dreading having to get through another day. \n\nAs for my appetite, I haven't really noticed much of a change there, honestly. It's mostly just the sleeping that's a problem.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant loss of energy or fatigue over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, like I just mentioned, I'm tired when I wake up because I'm not sleeping well. But other than that... I haven't noticed any real drop in my physical energy. I don't feel unusually fatigued during the day or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a significant reduction in self-esteem over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. I just... I keep telling myself I’m useless now. It's like all those years of taking care of my clients and providing for my family, it feels like none of that even counts anymore. And it's just because I can’t even handle this job right now. So, yeah, I guess you could say my confidence is pretty much gone. I just feel worthless.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of worthlessness and the thoughts of your family being better off without you are present most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say so. It's pretty much a constant thing. From the time I wake up in the morning to when I'm trying to fall asleep at night, those thoughts are just... they're just there. It doesn't really let up.\n\nDoctor: Would you say these feelings and thoughts have been present every day for the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's been every day. It's been going on for about eight months now. It all really started when the new regional manager came in and just started coming down on me, taking away accounts that I'd handled for years. Ever since then, it's just been every single day.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any psychomotor agitation or retardation over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: I'm not exactly sure what those words mean, to be honest. Um... if you're asking if I'm moving unusually slow, or if I'm, you know, restless and pacing around all the time... no, I haven't really noticed anything like that. Physically, I'm moving about the same as I always have.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of depression have caused you significant distress or impairment in your social, occupational, or other areas of functioning?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really has. At work, I actually missed an important client presentation recently. I just couldn't get it together. And at home... I've pulled back so much from everything. Honestly, my wife is the one who felt she had to bring me in here to see you. She said she didn't think I was safe carrying on like this anymore.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your depression is moderately severe, severe, or extremely severe, based on how much it affects your daily life and ability to function?\n\nPatient: I guess I would say it's severe. It's really affecting everything I do, like I said about work and my wife bringing me in. It's definitely not just a mild thing... it feels pretty severe to me.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in, even if it's just a little bit?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I used to look forward to a lot of things... bowling, working out in the garden, taking my wife to gallery shows, or even just going dancing. But now... I just don't get any enjoyment out of them. I mostly just sit there and make excuses not to go, because honestly, none of it feels worth the effort anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are not your own or that someone or something is controlling your mind?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. My thoughts are definitely my own. They're just... well, they're mostly pretty dark and heavy these days. But no, I don't feel like anyone or anything else is controlling my mind.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Yeah, eight months. It really started when the new regional manager took over. He just started coming down on me... taking away accounts that I’d handled for years.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Not anymore, no. I used to... well, I used to really look forward to things. Bowling, working out in the garden, taking my wife to gallery shows, or going dancing. But now... I just sit there. I have. I've been having these thoughts that maybe my wife and kids would just be better off if I didn't wake up in the morning. It's hard to say out loud, but... those thoughts really scare me. They scare me enough that I honestly just don't trust myself the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange or unusual thoughts, such as hearing voices or seeing things that others cannot, during these past eight months?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I haven't heard any voices or seen anything that isn't there. It's really just... just my own thoughts getting so dark. But no, no strange visions or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your appetite or sleep patterns over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, my sleep has been... well, it's been pretty bad. At night I just lie there staring at the ceiling, replaying work over and over in my head. And when I finally do get to sleep, I end up waking up around three or four in the morning. I wake up already worn out... just dreading having to get through another day. \n\nAs for my appetite, I haven't really noticed much of a change there, honestly. It's mostly just the sleeping that's a problem.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant loss of energy or fatigue over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, like I just mentioned, I'm tired when I wake up because I'm not sleeping well. But other than that... I haven't noticed any real drop in my physical energy. I don't feel unusually fatigued during the day or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a significant reduction in self-esteem over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. I just... I keep telling myself I’m useless now. It's like all those years of taking care of my clients and providing for my family, it feels like none of that even counts anymore. And it's just because I can’t even handle this job right now. So, yeah, I guess you could say my confidence is pretty much gone. I just feel worthless.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of worthlessness and the thoughts of your family being better off without you are present most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say so. It's pretty much a constant thing. From the time I wake up in the morning to when I'm trying to fall asleep at night, those thoughts are just... they're just there. It doesn't really let up.\n\nDoctor: Would you say these feelings and thoughts have been present every day for the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's been every day. It's been going on for about eight months now. It all really started when the new regional manager came in and just started coming down on me, taking away accounts that I'd handled for years. Ever since then, it's just been every single day.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any psychomotor agitation or retardation over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: I'm not exactly sure what those words mean, to be honest. Um... if you're asking if I'm moving unusually slow, or if I'm, you know, restless and pacing around all the time... no, I haven't really noticed anything like that. Physically, I'm moving about the same as I always have.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of depression have caused you significant distress or impairment in your social, occupational, or other areas of functioning?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really has. It's really affecting everything I do, like I said about work and my wife bringing me in. It's definitely not just a mild thing... it feels pretty severe to me.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in, even if it's just a little bit?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I find myself making excuses not to go out because, honestly, none of it feels worth the effort anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about harming yourself or wished you were dead during these past eight months?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I have. I've been having these thoughts that maybe my wife and kids would just be better off if I didn't wake up in the morning. It's hard to say out loud, but... those thoughts really scare me. They scare me enough that I honestly just don't trust myself the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange or unusual thoughts, such as hearing voices or seeing things that others cannot, during these past eight months?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. I haven't heard any voices or seen anything that isn't there. It's really just... just my own thoughts getting so dark. But no, no strange visions or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your appetite or sleep patterns over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, my sleep has been... well, it's been pretty bad. At night I just lie there staring at the ceiling, replaying work over and over in my head. And when I finally do get to sleep, I end up waking up around three or four in the morning. I wake up already worn out... just dreading having to get through another day. \n\nAs for my appetite, I haven't really noticed much of a change there, honestly. It's mostly just the sleeping that's a problem.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant loss of energy or fatigue over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, like I just mentioned, I'm tired when I wake up because I'm not sleeping well. But other than that... I haven't noticed any real drop in my physical energy. I don't feel unusually fatigued during the day or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a significant reduction in self-esteem over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. I just... I keep telling myself I’m useless now. It's like all those years of taking care of my clients and providing for my family, it feels like none of that even counts anymore. And it's just because I can’t even handle this job right now. So, yeah, I guess you could say my confidence is pretty much gone. I just feel worthless.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of worthlessness and the thoughts of your family being better off without you are present most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say so. It's pretty much a constant thing. From the time I wake up in the morning to when I'm trying to fall asleep at night, those thoughts are just... they're just there. It doesn't really let up.\n\nDoctor: Would you say these feelings and thoughts have been present every day for the past eight months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's been every day. It's been going on for about eight months now. It all really started when the new regional manager came in and just started coming down on me, taking away accounts that I'd handled for years. Ever since then, it's just been every single day.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any psychomotor agitation or retardation over the past eight months?\n\nPatient: I'm not exactly sure what those words mean, to be honest. Um... if you're asking if I'm moving unusually slow, or if I'm, you know, restless and pacing around all the time... no, I haven't really noticed anything like that. Physically, I'm moving about the same as I always have.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of depression have caused you significant distress or impairment in your social, occupational, or other areas of functioning?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really has. At work, I actually missed an important client presentation recently. I just couldn't get it together. And at home... I've pulled back so much from everything. Honestly, my wife is the one who felt she had to bring me in here to see you. She said she didn't think I was safe carrying on like this anymore.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your depression is moderately severe, severe, or extremely severe, based on how much it affects your daily life and ability to function?\n\nPatient: I guess I would say it's severe. It's really affecting everything I do, like I said about work and my wife bringing me in. It's definitely not just a mild thing... it feels pretty severe to me.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in, even if it's just a little bit?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I used to look forward to a lot of things... bowling, working out in the garden, taking my wife to gallery shows, or even just going dancing. But now... I just don't get any enjoyment out of them. I mostly just sit there and make excuses not to go, because honestly, none of it feels worth the effort anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are not your own or that someone or something is controlling your mind?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that. My thoughts are definitely my own. They're just... well, they're mostly pretty dark and heavy these days. But no, I don't feel like anyone or anything else is controlling my mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you made any plans to harm yourself when you've had those thoughts about your family being better off without you?\n\nPatient: I wouldn't say I have a specific plan, no. But... I’ve been having these thoughts that maybe my wife and kids would be better off if I just didn’t wake up. It gets pretty heavy. Honestly, those thoughts scare me enough that I really don’t trust myself the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I mean, I'm tired all the time, sure, but I don't think I'm actually moving any slower than I normally do.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience any pleasure even when good things happen to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say that's exactly it. I used to really look forward to things, you know? Like bowling, or working out in the garden. I used to take my wife to gallery shows, or we'd go dancing. But now... I just sit there. I make up excuses not to do any of it, because none of it even feels worth the effort anymore. Even if it's supposed to be a good time, I just don't get anything out of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving more slowly than you used to, even if it's just a little bit?\n\nPatient: No, like I said, I really haven't noticed anything like that. I'm just exhausted, but my movements seem pretty normal to me. I don't feel like I'm in slow motion or anything.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
